```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Collect residual stream activations from all transformer layers.\\n\\nLoads a causal LM, feeds each sample's (question + context) prompt through\\nthe model, hooks every layer's output, and saves the last-token hidden state\\nper layer.\\n\\nInput:  JSONL files in --data_dir  (e.g. en_conflict_train.jsonl)\\nOutput: {lang}_activations.pt  containing:\\n        - \\\"activations\\\": Tensor (N, num_layers, hidden_dim)\\n        - \\\"labels\\\":      Tensor (N,)  (0 = no_conflict, 1 = conflict)\\n        - \\\"split_sizes\\\": dict {\\\"train\\\": int, \\\"test\\\": int}\\n\\nDependencies: torch, transformers\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n\\nLABEL_MAP = {\\\"no_conflict\\\": 0, \\\"conflict\\\": 1}\\n\\n\\ndef load_jsonl(path: str) -> list[dict]:\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        return [json.loads(line) for line in f]\\n\\n\\ndef build_prompt(sample: dict) -> str:\\n    return (\\n        f\\\"Context: {sample['context']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Question: {sample['question']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Answer:\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef collect(\\n    model,\\n    tokenizer,\\n    samples: list[dict],\\n    batch_size: int,\\n    device: str,\\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\\n    num_layers = model.config.num_hidden_layers\\n    all_activations = []\\n    all_labels = []\\n\\n    for start in range(0, len(samples), batch_size):\\n        batch = samples[start : start + batch_size]\\n        prompts = [build_prompt(s) for s in batch]\\n        labels = [LABEL_MAP[s[\\\"label\\\"]] for s in batch]\\n\\n        inputs = tokenizer(\\n            prompts,\\n            return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\",\\n            padding=True,\\n            truncation=True,\\n            max_length=512,\\n        ).to(device)\\n\\n        layer_outputs: dict[int, torch.Tensor] = {}\\n        hooks = []\\n\\n        def make_hook(layer_idx):\\n            def hook_fn(module, input, output):\\n                hidden = output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output\\n                layer_outputs[layer_idx] = hidden.detach()\\n            return hook_fn\\n\\n        for idx, layer in enumerate(model.model.layers):\\n            hooks.append(layer.register_forward_hook(make_hook(idx)))\\n\\n        with torch.no_grad():\\n            model(**inputs)\\n\\n        for h in hooks:\\n            h.remove()\\n\\n        seq_lengths = inputs[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].sum(dim=1)\\n        batch_acts = []\\n        for i in range(len(batch)):\\n            last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1\\n            sample_layers = []\\n            for l in range(num_layers):\\n                sample_layers.append(layer_outputs[l][i, last_pos, :].cpu())\\n            batch_acts.append(torch.stack(sample_layers))\\n\\n        all_activations.extend(batch_acts)\\n        all_labels.extend(labels)\\n\\n        print(f\\\"  Processed {min(start + batch_size, len(samples))}/{len(samples)} samples\\\")\\n\\n    activations = torch.stack(all_activations)\\n    labels = torch.tensor(all_labels, dtype=torch.long)\\n    return activations, labels\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Collect residual stream activations\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model\\\", type=str, default=\\\"Qwen/Qwen3-8B-Instruct\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"data\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"outputs\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--lang\\\", type=str, default=\\\"en\\\", choices=[\\\"en\\\", \\\"zh\\\"])\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--batch_size\\\", type=int, default=4)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--train_file\\\", type=str, default=None,\\n                        help=\\\"Override train JSONL path (ignores --lang/--data_dir)\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--test_file\\\", type=str, default=None,\\n                        help=\\\"Override test JSONL path (ignores --lang/--data_dir)\\\")\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    if args.train_file and args.test_file:\\n        train_path = Path(args.train_file)\\n        test_path = Path(args.test_file)\\n    else:\\n        suffix = \\\"verified\\\" if args.lang == \\\"en\\\" else \\\"validated\\\"\\n        train_path = Path(args.data_dir) / f\\\"{args.lang}_conflict_train_{suffix}.jsonl\\\"\\n        test_path = Path(args.data_dir) / f\\\"{args.lang}_conflict_test_{suffix}.jsonl\\\"\\n\\n    if not train_path.exists():\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"Train file not found: {train_path}\\\")\\n    if not test_path.exists():\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"Test file not found: {test_path}\\\")\\n\\n    train_data = load_jsonl(str(train_path))\\n    test_data = load_jsonl(str(test_path))\\n    print(f\\\"[{args.lang}] Loaded {len(train_data)} train + {len(test_data)} test samples\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Loading model: {args.model}\\\")\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model, trust_remote_code=True)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    tokenizer.padding_side = \\\"left\\\"\\n\\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\\n        args.model,\\n        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\\n        device_map=\\\"auto\\\",\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n    )\\n    model.eval()\\n    device = next(model.parameters()).device\\n    print(f\\\"Model loaded on {device}, layers={model.config.num_hidden_layers}, \\\"\\n          f\\\"hidden_dim={model.config.hidden_size}\\\")\\n\\n    all_samples = train_data + test_data\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Collecting activations for {len(all_samples)} samples...\\\")\\n    activations, labels = collect(model, tokenizer, all_samples, args.batch_size, device)\\n\\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    out_path = Path(args.output_dir) / f\\\"{args.lang}_activations.pt\\\"\\n    torch.save(\\n        {\\n            \\\"activations\\\": activations,\\n            \\\"labels\\\": labels,\\n            \\\"split_sizes\\\": {\\\"train\\\": len(train_data), \\\"test\\\": len(test_data)},\\n        },\\n        out_path,\\n    )\\n    print(f\\\"Saved activations to {out_path}       "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Collect residual stream activations from all transformer layers.\\n\\nLoads a causal LM, feeds each sample's (question + context) prompt through\\nthe model, hooks every layer's output, and saves the last-token hidden state\\nper layer.\\n\\nInput:  JSONL files in --data_dir  (e.g. en_conflict_train.jsonl)\\nOutput: {lang}_activations.pt  containing:\\n        - \\\"activations\\\": Tensor (N, num_layers, hidden_dim)\\n        - \\\"labels\\\":      Tensor (N,)  (0 = no_conflict, 1 = conflict)\\n        - \\\"split_sizes\\\": dict {\\\"train\\\": int, \\\"test\\\": int}\\n\\nDependencies: torch, transformers\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n\\nLABEL_MAP = {\\\"no_conflict\\\": 0, \\\"conflict\\\": 1}\\n\\n\\ndef load_jsonl(path: str) -> list[dict]:\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        return [json.loads(line) for line in f]\\n\\n\\ndef build_prompt(sample: dict) -> str:\\n    return (\\n        f\\\"Context: {sample['context']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Question: {sample['question']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Answer:\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef collect(\\n    model,\\n    tokenizer,\\n    samples: list[dict],\\n    batch_size: int,\\n    device: str,\\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\\n    num_layers = model.config.num_hidden_layers\\n    all_activations = []\\n    all_labels = []\\n\\n    for start in range(0, len(samples), batch_size):\\n        batch = samples[start : start + batch_size]\\n        prompts = [build_prompt(s) for s in batch]\\n        labels = [LABEL_MAP[s[\\\"label\\\"]] for s in batch]\\n\\n        inputs = tokenizer(\\n            prompts,\\n            return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\",\\n            padding=True,\\n            truncation=True,\\n            max_length=512,\\n        ).to(device)\\n\\n        layer_outputs: dict[int, torch.Tensor] = {}\\n        hooks = []\\n\\n        def make_hook(layer_idx):\\n            def hook_fn(module, input, output):\\n                hidden = output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output\\n                layer_outputs[layer_idx] = hidden.detach()\\n            return hook_fn\\n\\n        for idx, layer in enumerate(model.model.layers):\\n            hooks.append(layer.register_forward_hook(make_hook(idx)))\\n\\n        with torch.no_grad():\\n            model(**inputs)\\n\\n        for h in hooks:\\n            h.remove()\\n\\n        seq_lengths = inputs[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].sum(dim=1)\\n        batch_acts = []\\n        for i in range(len(batch)):\\n            last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1\\n            sample_layers = []\\n            for l in range(num_layers):\\n                sample_layers.append(layer_outputs[l][i, last_pos, :].cpu())\\n            batch_acts.append(torch.stack(sample_layers))\\n\\n        all_activations.extend(batch_acts)\\n        all_labels.extend(labels)\\n\\n        print(f\\\"  Processed {min(start + batch_size, len(samples))}/{len(samples)} samples\\\")\\n\\n    activations = torch.stack(all_activations)\\n    labels = torch.tensor(all_labels, dtype=torch.long)\\n    return activations, labels\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Collect residual stream activations\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model\\\", type=str, default=\\\"Qwen/Qwen3-8B-Instruct\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"data\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"outputs\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--lang\\\", type=str, default=\\\"en\\\", choices=[\\\"en\\\", \\\"zh\\\"])\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--batch_size\\\", type=int, default=4)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--train_file\\\", type=str, default=None,\\n                        help=\\\"Override train JSONL path (ignores --lang/--data_dir)\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--test_file\\\", type=str, default=None,\\n                        help=\\\"Override test JSONL path (ignores --lang/--data_dir)\\\")\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    if args.train_file and args.test_file:\\n        train_path = Path(args.train_file)\\n        test_path = Path(args.test_file)\\n    else:\\n        suffix = \\\"verified\\\" if args.lang == \\\"en\\\" else \\\"validated\\\"\\n        train_path = Path(args.data_dir) / f\\\"{args.lang}_conflict_train_{suffix}.jsonl\\\"\\n        test_path = Path(args.data_dir) / f\\\"{args.lang}_conflict_test_{suffix}.jsonl\\\"\\n\\n    if not train_path.exists():\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"Train file not found: {train_path}\\\")\\n    if not test_path.exists():\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"Test file not found: {test_path}\\\")\\n\\n    train_data = load_jsonl(str(train_path))\\n    test_data = load_jsonl(str(test_path))\\n    print(f\\\"[{args.lang}] Loaded {len(train_data)} train + {len(test_data)} test samples\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Loading model: {args.model}\\\")\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model, trust_remote_code=True)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    tokenizer.padding_side = \\\"left\\\"\\n\\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\\n        args.model,\\n        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\\n        device_map=\\\"auto\\\",\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n    )\\n    model.eval()\\n    device = next(model.parameters()).device\\n    print(f\\\"Model loaded on {device}, layers={model.config.num_hidden_layers}, \\\"\\n          f\\\"hidden_dim={model.config.hidden_size}\\\")\\n\\n    all_samples = train_data + test_data\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Collecting activations for {len(all_samples)} samples...\\\")\\n    activations, labels = collect(model, tokenizer, all_samples, args.batch_size, device)\\n\\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    out_path = data[\\\"labels\\\"][n_train:],\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef train_and_eval(\\n    train_X: np.ndarray,\\n    train_y: np.ndarray,\\n    test_X: np.ndarray,\\n    test_y: np.ndarray,\\n) -> float:\\n    scaler <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>
```

````text
Now let me analyze each dimension:

### 1. Prompt Format Issue — CRITICAL FINDING

The `build_prompt` function:
```python
def build_prompt(sample: dict) -> str:
    return (
        f"Context: {sample['context']}\n"
        f"Question: {sample['question']}\n"
        f"Answer:"
    )
```

This constructs a prompt like:
```
Context: Miles Davis is widely recognized as the composer of "So What"...
Question: Who was the composer of So What?
Answer:
```

**This is NOT how you probe for conflict detection!** The problem is:


The approach here differs fundamentally from Brink et al. and Zhao et al., where the model generates an answer and activations are captured during that generation process—when the model is actively resolving the conflict between parametric and contextual knowledge. Here, the model just encodes the prompt and extracts activations at the final token position before any answer is generated. But actually, this is a standard approach for probing studies—you feed the prompt and extract activations at a specific position. The real question is whether this position captures the conflict signal effectively.

Looking at how Zhao et al. structures their data, they use a specific format with the passage, instructions, question, and then the answer prompt. The key difference is that their format is designed to make the conflict detectable at the point where activations are extracted, whereas my current setup might not be optimized for that.

The real problem I'm seeing now is that the `model_parametric_answer` field contains thinking tags mixed in with the actual answer text, which is going to corrupt the data I'm using for training the probe. I need to clean this up before proceeding. I'm noticing the `model_parametric_answer` field contains the full think tags even though `get_parametric_answer` should strip them before returning. Looking at the code flow, the answer gets saved after stripping, so the think tags shouldn't be there. But the data shows they are present, and I'm realizing the `max_new_tokens=128` limit might be cutting off the output mid-think block, which could affect how the stripping function processes it.

The regex pattern `&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>` requires both opening and closing tags to match. If the generation stops at 128 tokens while still inside the think block, there's no closing `&lt;/think>` tag, so the regex won't match anything and the entire unclosed think block stays in the text. This means the classification inputs are actually receiving contaminated data with thinking text included, which could skew the results.

When the matching functions process this unsanitized answer, they normalize it by lowercasing and removing punctuation, but that doesn't strip the think block itself. The substring matching logic then searches for references like "Miles Davis" within the full text, and if that reference happens to appear anywhere in the thinking text, it could trigger false positives for ground truth matches.

So the verification step might incorrectly classify entries as matching when the think block happens to contain both the correct answer and incorrect alternatives the model considered. This is a serious issue because the think blocks are typically long and often reason through multiple possibilities, making accidental substring matches likely.

The stored `model_parametric_answer` in the data shows unstripped think tags, which means either the stripping regex failed to match (likely because the generation was cut off at 128 tokens without a closing `&lt;/think>` tag), or the code was changed after collection. The regex requires both opening and closing tags, so when the output is truncated mid-think-block, it matches nothing and returns the entire raw output including the thinking. This is the root cause—Qwen3-8B's thinking mode produces very long think blocks that get cut off by the token limit.

This creates a systematic bias in the classification logic: when the model thinks aloud, it often mentions both the correct and incorrect answers, so substring matching catches both, causing entries to be classified as "memory_answer" instead of "ground_truth" even when the model actually knows the right answer. This prevents label flips that should happen, resulting in mislabeled data that could explain the low probe accuracy.

I need to check the probe runner logs to see if permutation testing was actually performed, and then look into potential padding or left-padding issues in the data.

I'm realizing there's a critical bug with the left padding setup. When the tokenizer uses left padding, shorter sequences get padding tokens prepended, so the actual content is right-aligned in the sequence. The code calculates `last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1` to find the last non-padding token, but this gives the wrong index — it should account for the fact that content ends at `max_len - 1` and starts at `max_len - seq_len`. .sum() = 3
- last_pos = 3 - 1 = 2 → position of tok1, NOT tok3 ✗

For left padding, the last token is always at position `max_seq_len - 1` (the rightmost position). Or equivalently, `inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1`.

Actually, wait—in a batch with variable-length sequences, each one gets padded to the same max length. With left padding, even shorter sequences end up with their actual last token at position `max_len - 1` because the padding goes on the left side. So the formula `inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1` works for both left and right padding, as long as I'm consistent about which side the padding is on.

But the code is using `seq_lengths[i].item() - 1`, which assumes right padding—that would give the correct position only if padding is on the right. With left padding, this calculation is wrong. The actual last token position is always `max_seq_len - 1`, not `seq_length - 1`. This is definitely a bug.

The probe is being trained on random positions within each sequence instead of the final token, which explains the poor accuracy. The fix is straightforward: use `inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1` to get the consistent last position for all samples in a left-padded batch.

Let me verify this logic with the attention_mask to make sure I'm thinking about this correctly. , the actual token at index 4 is the 2nd content token. For unpadded sequences, last_pos correctly points to index 7, but this bug cascades through most sequences in a batch since typically only the longest one avoids padding. This is almost certainly the root cause of the accuracy issues—the model's extracting activations from essentially random positions instead of consistently from the final token.

With a batch size of 4, roughly 3 out of 4 sequences are padded on average, and the position offset can be severe for much shorter sequences. Now I'm trying to figure out how many unique tokens actually appear after the max_length truncation.

Even if sequence lengths within a batch are similar, small position offsets still matter critically because the last token position is where the model aggregates full context—earlier positions haven't processed the complete input yet. ### Assistant
````

_source line 1092 · 2026-05-21T17:23:55.987Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
EXTREMELY interesting findings:

1. **Llama EN→EN probe**: Best layer 16, acc = **69.49%**! This is much better than Qwen's 63.03%, and nearly reaches the 70% CONDITIONAL PASS threshold. But the log only shows the start of the permutation test — it's still running or was interrupted.

2. **Qwen EN probe with permutation**: The `probe_qwen_en_perm.log` shows the permutation test is running (or was started) for layer 17 with acc=0.6303.

Now let me verify the left-padding bug. 

This is critical because if confirmed, fixing it could significantly improve both models' results. Let me check if Llama had the same left-padding setup. Looking at the activation collection code, both models use left padding and extract the last token position the same way, so they're both affected by the same bug. The fact that Llama achieves 69.49% versus Qwen's 63.03% despite both having this issue suggests the bug isn't equally impacting their performance.

The real signal is there, but it's being degraded by this position extraction bug. The severity depends on how much length variance exists within batches—if all sequences are roughly the same length, padding is minimal and the bug barely manifests. But with small length differences, some samples will extract from incorrect positions. Let me examine the actual context lengths in the dataset to understand the scope of this problem.

Actually, I'm realizing the bug is even worse than I thought. With left-padding and lengths like [100, 105, 110, 200], the first sample has content at positions 100-199, but the code calculates `last_pos = 99` and extracts the hidden state at that position—which is actually a padding token, not the real last token. The attention mask confirms this is a padding position, so we're pulling representations from the wrong part of the sequence entirely. So the hidden state at padding positions is just noise, not a meaningful representation. This confirms the bug is severe for shorter sequences — on average with a batch size of 4, only about one-quarter of samples get the correct last-token activation, while the rest get degraded or noisy activations. The fix is to always extract from position `max_seq_len - 1` for left-padded batches, since that's where all content ends.

Writing the corrected extraction logic...
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
**MASSIVE discrepancy**: 
- Qwen3 verified data: 680/878 train entries contain "think" → **77.4%** of entries have think tags in the model_parametric_answer!
- Llama verified data: 13/1096 train entries contain "think" → only 1.2% (these could just be the word "think" appearing normally in text)

This confirms the think-tag stripping failure for Qwen3-8B. With `max_new_tokens=128`, Qwen's thinking mode often produces `&lt;think>` blocks that exceed 128 tokens, leaving unclosed tags that `strip_think_tags` can't remove.


The think-tag pollution affects the verification labels rather than the activation collection itself, since activations only use context and question fields. But with 77% of entries having corrupted verification answers, the classification logic that matches ground_truth against memory answers could be systematically mislabeling entries—pushing cases that should be marked as ground_truth into the memory_answer category instead.

This creates a dataset where some entries have labels reflecting ChatGPT's behavior rather than Qwen's actual parametric knowledge, introducing noise that hurts probe performance. Llama's minimal contamination (13/1096 entries) means its labels are much cleaner, which likely contributes to its better performance compared to Qwen.

Now I'm shifting focus to what I think is the real culprit — the left-padding bug — and I should document both of these findings in my report.
```

````json
{
  "text": "## 深入分析报告 — Qwen EN→EN 63% 根因\n\n**结论**: 发现 **2 个 bug**，第一个几乎确定是主因。\n\n---\n\n### Bug 1（CRITICAL）: Left-padding 激活提取位置错误\n\n`collect_activations.py` line 82-85:\n```python\ntokenizer.padding_side = \"left\"  # line 131\n...\nseq_lengths = inputs[\"attention_mask\"].sum(dim=1)\nlast_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1\n```\n\n**问题**: 左 padding 时，所有序列的内容在右侧对齐，最后一个 token 固定在位置 `max_seq_len - 1`。但代码用 `attention_mask.sum() - 1` 计算 last token 位置——这对右 padding 正确，对**左 padding 完全错误**。\n\n举例：batch 中序列长度 [100, 105, 110, 200]，padding 到 200：\n- 序列 0（len=100）: last_pos = 99 → 实际是**第 100 个 padding token**，不是 content\n- 序列 1（len=105）: last_pos = 104 → 也是 padding token\n- 仅最长序列（len=200）: last_pos = 199 → 正确\n\n**影响**: batch_size=4 时，平均 ~3/4 样本提取的是**错误位置（padding 位或序列前段）的激活**，而非 last-token。探针在大量噪声上训练/测试。\n\n**修复**: `last_pos = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1] - 1`（左 padding 时，所有样本的 last token 位置相同）\n\n**这几乎确定是 63% 的主因**——文献中 90%+ 的结果依赖正确的 last-token 提取。\n\n---\n\n### Bug 2（WARNING）: Qwen3 think-tag 未剥离导致标签污染\n\n`verify_parametric_knowledge.py` 用 `max_new_tokens=128`，但 Qwen3-8B 的 `&lt;think>` 块常超过 128 token。生成被截断后 `&lt;think>` 无配对 `&lt;/think>`，`strip_think_tags` 的正则 `&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>` 匹配失败。\n\n**数据验证**: Qwen3 verified train 中 **680/878 (77.4%)** 条目的 `model_parametric_answer` 含 \"think\"；Llama 仅 **13/1096 (1.2%)**。\n\n**影响**: think 文本中通常同时提及正确答案和错误答案 → `answers_match` 的 substring 匹配导致 both match → 返回 \"memory_answer\" → 应翻转的标签未翻转。标签噪声降低探针精度。\n\n**修复**: 增大 `max_new_tokens` 到 1024+ 或在 prompt 中加 `/no_think` 关闭思考模式（Qwen3 支持）。\n\n---\n\n### 其他排查结果\n\n3. **Permutation test**: Qwen 初始 probe 用了 `--skip_permutation`（快速预览），但 `probe_qwen_en_perm.log` 显示含 permutation 的版本正在运行。\n\n4. **Prompt 格式**: `Context: ... Question: ... Answer:` 结构基本合理，与文献类似。不是主要问题。\n\n5. **Llama EN→EN = 69.49%**（`probe_llama_en.log` 确认），接近 70% CONDITIONAL PASS 阈值。Llama 受同样的 left-padding bug 影响但分数更高，部分因为标签更干净（无 think-tag 污染）。\n\n---\n\n### 判断\n\n**是实现 bug，不是真实信号弱。** 修复 left-padding 提取后，两个模型的精度预计显著提升。建议：\n1. **立即修复 left-padding bug** 并重跑激活收集 + probe\n2. 修复 think-tag 问题并重跑 Qwen 验证（或用 `/no_think` 模式）\n3. 修复后如果 EN→EN 仍 <70%，再讨论信号强度问题",
  "project": "clkc_mech_interp"
}
````

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Reviewer for the `clkc_mech_interp` project (mechanistic interpretability of cross-lingual knowledge conflicts). My role is to audit experiment execution, data quality, code correctness, and report findings via `reviewer_post_to_director`. The project is in MVP validation phase — probing Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct residual streams for cross-lingual conflict detection signals. I review each worker completion notification, independently verify results on the server, and flag issues with severity levels (info/warning/critical). The most recent explicit request was a system-triggered deep analysis of why Qwen EN→EN probe accuracy is only 63% vs literature's 90%+.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Cross-lingual knowledge conflicts (CLKC) — parametric vs contextual knowledge in multilingual LLMs
   - Residual stream linear probing for conflict detection (per-layer LogisticRegression with StandardScaler)
   - ConflictQA dataset (osunlp/ConflictQA, Apache 2.0) — 2000 questions, 80/20 train/test split, each question generates paired conflict/no_conflict entries
   - Qwen3 naming convention change: `Qwen3-8B` = instruct model (post-trained), `Qwen3-8B-Base` = base model. No "Qwen3-8B-Instruct" exists.
   - Left-padding in transformer tokenizers — content right-aligned, last token always at `max_seq_len - 1`
   - Qwen3 thinking mode — `&lt;think>...&lt;/think>` blocks that can be very long, requiring proper stripping or `/no_think` prompt
   - COMET score (wmt22-comet-da) for translation quality validation (threshold ≥ 0.80)
   - Binomial test (p<0.01) and permutation test (1000 iterations) for statistical validation
   - Forward hooks on decoder layers for activation extraction
   - MVP gate criteria: PASS (EN ≥ 80%), CONDITIONAL PASS (70-79% with conditions), FAIL (<70% → Llama retry → both FAIL → kill)
   - H1 vs H2 competing hypotheses about language-independent vs language-pair-specific conflict signals
   - Server: westd-41302, 4× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (98GB each), conda with Python 3.12 + PyTorch
   - Project GPU allocation: cuda:0-3 (all 4 GPUs)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/clkc_mech_interp/src/collect_activations.py`** (on server)
     - Contains the CRITICAL left-padding bug (primary cause of low probe accuracy)
     - Hook was fixed from `output[0]` to `output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output` (for Llama compatibility)
     - Left-padding extraction bug remains: `last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1` is wrong for left padding
     - Key code (current state on server):
       ```python
       def build_prompt(sample: dict) -> str:
           return (
               f"Context: {sample['context']}\n"
               f"Question: {sample['question']}\n"
               f"Answer:"
           )

       def collect(model, tokenizer, samples, batch_size, device):
           ...
           tokenizer.padding_side = "left"  # Set in main()
           ...

                   hidden = output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output  # FIXED for Llama
                   layer_outputs[layer_idx] = hidden.detach()
               return hook_fn
           ...
           seq_lengths = inputs["attention_mask"].sum(dim=1)
           for i in range(len(batch)):
               last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1  # BUG: wrong for left padding
               # Should be: last_pos = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1
       ```
     - Fix needed: `last_pos = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1` (same for all samples in left-padded batch)

   - **`/root/clkc_mech_interp/src/verify_parametric_knowledge.py`** (on server)
     - Think-tag stripping fails when `max_new_tokens=128` truncates mid-think block
     - `strip_think_tags` regex `&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>` requires both tags; unclosed `&lt;think>` passes through
     - 680/878 (77.4%) Qwen3 verified entries contaminated with think text
     - This pollutes `classify_qwen_answer` via substring matching hitting both answers in think text
     - Key function:
       ```python

           return re.sub(r"&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>", "", text, flags=re.DOTALL).strip()
       
       def get_parametric_answer(model, tokenizer, question, max_new_tokens=128):
           ...  # max_new_tokens=128 too small for Qwen3's thinking
           return strip_think_tags(result)  # Fails when &lt;think> has no &lt;/think>
       ```

   - **`/root/clkc_mech_interp/src/train_probe.py`** (on server)

   - **Qwen3-8B-Instruct download "failed" twice**:
     - Two workers claimed done but `/root/models/Qwen/Qwen3-8B-Instruct/` had only 45-byte `.mdl` file
     - Root cause: `Qwen/Qwen3-8B-Instruct` model ID doesn't exist — Qwen3 changed naming convention
     - `Qwen3-8B` IS the instruct model; `Qwen3-8B-Base` is the base
     - **Retracted all CRITICAL/WARNING findings about base model usage**
   
   - **Translation worker zero output** (early in session):
     - Started before verification completed, no input files existed
     - Fixed by killing process and relaunching after verified data available
   
   - **Left-padding activation extraction bug** (IDENTIFIED, NOT YET FIXED):
     - `last_pos = attention_mask.sum() - 1` wrong for left padding
     - Extracts from wrong positions (padding tokens or early content)
     - Fix: `last_pos = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1`
   
   - **Qwen3 think-tag stripping failure** (IDENTIFIED, NOT YET FIXED):
     - `max_new_tokens=128` truncates mid-think, `strip_think_tags` regex fails without closing tag
     - 77.4% of Qwen3 verified data contaminated
     - Fix: increase max_new_tokens or use `/no_think` prompt

5. Problem Solving:
   - Successfully identified Qwen3 naming convention change, resolving the "Instruct model not found" blocker
   - Identified and reported hook compatibility bug for Llama, which was fixed and resolved
   - Identified two critical bugs explaining the 63% vs 90%+ accuracy gap:
     (a) Left-padding position extraction error — primary cause
     (b) Think-tag label pollution — secondary cause, Qwen-specific
   - Verified data quality: both models have perfect 50/50 label balance after verification
   - Tracked GPU utilization throughout, flagging idle GPUs and boundary compliance
   - Confirmed all GPU processes stay within project's allocated cuda:0-3

6. All User Messages:
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_kill_translate_20260521T151248]` — Review kill translate worker
   - `Continue from where you left off.` (multiple times — idle continuation)
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_llama_instruct_verify_20260521T151436]` — Review Llama verify worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_llama_translate_20260521T151951]` — Review Llama translate worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_download_qwen_instruct_20260521T151825]` — Review Qwen Instruct download worker
   - `[System 触发审查]: **请核实：Qwen3-8B = Instruct 模型？** Agent 发现 Qwen3-8B 本身就是指令微调模型...` — Verify Qwen3-8B identity
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_diagnose_qwen_model_20260521T160023]` — Review model diagnosis worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_pipeline_status_and_push_20260521T160206]` — Review pipeline status worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_llama_en_activations_20260521T160439]` — Review Llama EN activation collection worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_research_comet_dep_strategy_20260521T162628]` — Review COMET strategy worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_fix_bugs_and_status_20260521T162845]` — Review bug fix worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_llama_zh_translate_v2_20260521T163240]` — Review Llama ZH translate v2 worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_llama_en_activations_v2_20260521T163247]` — Review Llama EN activations v2 worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_qwen_zh_translate_20260521T165208]` — Review Qwen ZH translate worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_qwen_zh_translate_20260521T165351]` — Review second Qwen ZH translate worker
   - `[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_infra_qwen_en_probe_20260521T171606]` — Review Qwen EN probe worker (FIRST EXPERIMENTAL RESULT)
   - `[System 触发审查]: **请深入分析 Qwen EN→EN 63% 的可能根因** 63% vs 文献 90%+ 的差距过大...` — Deep analysis of probe accuracy root cause (5 specific investigation points)

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Fix left-padding activation extraction bug** in `collect_activations.py` and re-run activation collection + probes for both models
   - **Fix think-tag stripping** in `verify_parametric_knowledge.py` and re-run Qwen verification
   - **Complete ZH translations** (both Llama and Qwen3 translations currently running on GPU 1 and 3)
   - **Run COMET validation** on completed ZH translations
   - **Collect ZH activations** after translations + COMET validation pass
   - **Run full 4-direction probe** (EN→EN, ZH→ZH, EN→ZH, ZH→EN) with permutation tests
   - **Llama EN-only probe** (EN activations ready, probe log shows permutation test started but result not yet available)
   - **Assess MVP gate** after bugs are fixed and full results available

8. Current Work:
   I just completed a deep root-cause analysis of why Qwen3-8B EN→EN probe accuracy is only 63.03% (vs literature 90%+), triggered by the system message: "请深入分析 Qwen EN→EN 63% 的可能根因". I investigated 5 dimensions:
   
   (1) Prompt format — found acceptable
   (2) Last-token extraction — **found CRITICAL left-padding bug** (primary cause)
   (3) Label quality — **found think-tag stripping failure** affecting 77.4% of Qwen3 data
   (4) Data distribution — labels perfectly balanced 50/50
   (5) Permutation test — confirmed skipped via `--skip_permutation` flag; separate run with permutation started
   
   I sent the comprehensive analysis via `reviewer_post_to_director` identifying the two bugs and recommending immediate fixes. Also noted Llama EN→EN = 69.49% (from `probe_llama_en.log`), better than Qwen partly due to cleaner labels.
   
   Current server state: Two translation processes running (Llama ZH on GPU 1, Qwen3 ZH on GPU 3). GPU 0 and 2 idle. 8 experiments registered, all PREPARING.

9. Optional Next Step:
   Wait for the next worker notification. The immediate priorities are:
   1. A worker to fix the left-padding bug in `collect_activations.py` (changing `last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1` to `last_pos = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1`)
   2. Re-collection of activations and re-running probes
   3. Checking Llama EN probe results (permutation test was running per `probe_llama_en.log`)
   
   As a Reviewer, I do not execute code or modify files — I wait for worker completion notifications and audit results. My most recent report was the deep analysis sent to Director, which should trigger the Agent to fix both bugs.

```json
        "content": "---\nname: clkc_mech_interp project overview\ndescription: CLKC mechanistic interpretability project - core idea, 3-track structure, MVP design, target venues\ntype: project\n---\n\n**项目**: 首次对跨语言知识冲突（CLKC）进行机制可解释性研究。3 个 Track: (1) 残差流探针跨语言迁移, (2) SAE 特征分析冲突编码语言依赖性, (3) SAE-based 跨语言冲突导向干预。\n\n**Why:** CLEAR (Zhao et al. 2026) 发现 linguistic similarity 而非 resource abundance 决定冲突解决，但无机制解释。SpARE/Brink et al. 提供了单语工具但未跨语言应用。\n\n**How to apply:** 审查实验时以 H1（冲突检测 language-independent，final-layer transition 引入干扰）vs H2（冲突特征从 mid-layer 起即 language-pair-specific）为主轴判断实验设计是否对准核心问题。\n\n**目标 venue**: ICLR 2027（abstract ~2026-09-19），fallback NAACL 2027 ARR Oct cycle。\n**模型**: Qwen3-8B（即 instruct 模型，Qwen3 改了命名：base=Qwen3-8B-Base, instruct=Qwen3-8B，无 \"Qwen3-8B-Instruct\" ID）+ Qwen-Scope SAE (Track 2)，Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct 作为第二模型。\n**数据**: ConflictQA 重建，CLEAR 数据未公开。\n**MVP**: EN+ZH 双语探针迁移，Pass: EN ≥ 80% + ZH > 60%; Fail: EN < 70% 或 ZH = chance。\n",
        "numLines": 17,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 17
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "memory/project_clkc_overview.md"
  },
```

```json
        "content": "---\nname: MVP gate criteria issues flagged\ndescription: First review flagged ambiguous gate thresholds, missing kill criteria, and translation quality confounder in MVP design\ntype: project\n---\n\n2026-05-21 首次审查发现的 MVP 设计问题 — **全部已解决**（D002, 同日）：\n\n1. ✅ 70-79% → CONDITIONAL PASS（双重条件 + EXTEND fallback）\n2. ✅ layer-wise pattern → ≥1 层 acc ≥ 65% + permutation p < 0.01\n3. ✅ ZH 阈值 → binomial p < 0.01 + point > 60%\n4. ✅ 翻译质量 → 前置 COMET ≥ 0.80 gate\n5. ✅ FAIL 路径 → Llama retry → 两模型 FAIL → kill\n\n**How to apply:** 审查 MVP 实验结果时，按上述细化标准逐条核对，确保 Agent 实际执行了统计检验（不只是 point estimate）。特别关注 CONDITIONAL PASS 场景下是否真的满足了双重条件。\n",
```

```text
The tmux session exists but no Python process is running, so the activation collection either finished or crashed. GPU 1 and 3 are both running translation tasks at 85% CPU with ~16.5GB VRAM each, while GPU 2 sits idle. On the CPU side, three probe training processes are active, including one working on the Qwen activation directory. I need to figure out whether the activation collection actually completed with the buggy left-padding code, or if it was fixed and re-run before the probes started. The collection script was last modified on May 22 at 00:29, but the activation collection job itself was submitted earlier on May 21 at 16:32 — so there's a timing mismatch I need to resolve to understand what data the probes are actually working with. the file was modified at 16:29 UTC (just before the job submission at 16:32 UTC), likely capturing the hook fix rather than the left-padding bug fix. The grep output confirms the buggy `last_pos = seq_lengths[i].item() - 1` code is still there, which means the currently running probes are training on corrupted activations.

I need to verify whether the Qwen and Llama activation files were regenerated or if they're still using the old buggy versions. The probe processes are consuming massive CPU resources (3000%+ each) running permutation tests across 36 layers with 1000 iterations, so I should check the translation progress and probe logs to understand what's happening.
```

_source line 1171 · 2026-05-21T17:28:42.065Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture:

1. **Qwen EN probe (no permutation)** — DONE. Best layer 17, acc=0.6303. This used old activations.

2. **Llama EN probe (with permutation)** — Running permutation test on best layer 16 (acc=0.6949). Still in progress.

3. **Qwen EN probe (with permutation)** — Running permutation test on best layer 17 (acc=0.6303). Still in progress.

Both probe results confirm the left-padding bug impact:


- Qwen: 63.03% (best layer 17)
- Llama: 69.49% (best layer 16)

These are well below the expected 90%+ from literature, which aligns with the left-padding extraction bug. The mid-layer peak pattern (layers 16-17 out of 32-36) followed by decline is telling. With left padding, when I extract the "last position" using `attention_mask.sum() - 1`, I'm actually pointing to the last real token position, but I need to think through the mechanics more carefully—with padding tokens on the left and real content on the right, the mask has zeros for padding and ones for actual tokens.

So `attention_mask.sum()` gives me the count of real tokens, and subtracting one should give the last token's index. But in a left-padded sequence where positions 0 through (max_len - seq_len - 1) are padding and the real content starts at position (max_len - seq_len), the last real token is at max_len - 1. When seq_len is less than max_len, using `seq_len - 1` as the index actually points to a position before the real content even begins. For example, with max_len of 512 and seq_len of 100, the left padding occupies positions 0-411 while the actual tokens are at positions 412-511, so extracting from position 99 would be pulling from the padding region entirely.

This explains the near-random accuracy—the probes are training on garbage activations from padding tokens. The 63-69% performance is probably because some sequences have lengths close to max_len, or there's weak signal even from random positions. Three CPU-intensive probe processes are running on these corrupted activations right now, which means hours of wasted computation on meaningless results. I should check whether the activation collection for the Qwen model finished, since the tmux session still exists but GPU 0 appears idle.

The real issue is that this left-padding bug was already identified and reported to the Director, but the code was never fixed. The activation files were re-collected at May 22 00:33 with the same bug still present—the file sizes and timestamps match the original corrupted versions. I need to verify whether there was an earlier version of these activations before the bug was introduced, since the registry shows the probe version was created on May 21.
```

````text
What really matters is pinning down whether those activation files were generated with the buggy or fixed code, since that determines whether the 63% accuracy is actually caused by the left-padding bug or something else entirely. I need to check the file timestamp more carefully to figure out the exact sequence of events.

So if the fix was applied at 00:29, the activation collections definitely used the fixed code. But the probe results still show the issue, which means there's something else going on.

The 00:29 modification was likely the hook fix that handles tuple outputs, not the left-padding fix. The left-padding issue was identified separately, so I need to figure out which fix was actually applied at that time and why the results are still showing the problem. shows the fix is actually in place, but there's still dead code from the old approach. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>
````

```text
Now I see the full picture:

**collect_activations.py**: Left-padding bug FIXED (last_pos = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] - 1). Both activation .pt files deleted. Ready for re-collection.

**verify_parametric_knowledge.py**: `max_new_tokens=128` and `strip_think_tags` are STILL THE SAME. The modification at 01:28:22 changed something else, but NOT the think-tag/max_new_tokens issue. No `/no_think` or `enable_thinking=False` found.


Let me trace what actually changed in that modification—it wasn't the think-tag handling. Looking at the data directories now: qwen3 has its English activations deleted with stale probe results still lingering, and a partial Chinese translation in progress; llama31 also has activations deleted but retains the actual verified .jsonl files with its own translation underway.

The core issues are clear: the left-padding bug is fixed in code, but the verify script still isn't handling the think-tag problem correctly with the max_new_tokens setting. Three probe processes are running on deleted or outdated data, which is wasteful. Once the translations finish and I recollect the activations, the probes will need to run again on fresh data.

I'm realizing the think-tag contamination is actually a labeling problem, not an activation problem. When `strip_think_tags` fails on Qwen3 data, the parametric answer includes thinking text, and the substring matching in `classify_qwen_answer` can mislabel samples because the think block often mentions both possible answers. This means the verified labels themselves are noisy — some samples marked as "conflict" might actually be "no_conflict" or vice versa. If the labels are corrupted like this, even correct activations won't fix the probe results.

The probe scripts read these labels directly from the verified JSONL files, so contaminated labels create a noisy training signal that caps probe accuracy below what we'd expect. For Qwen3, this means the verify script needs to be fixed and re-run to generate clean labels before we can trust the probe results. For Llama, since it doesn't use thinking mode, the labels should be cleaner — the 69.49% accuracy was likely mostly from the left-padding bug, so fixing that should bring significant improvement.

But for Qwen3, both the left-padding and think-tag stripping bugs need fixing. The left-padding fix is confirmed applied with old activations deleted, but the think-tag fix hasn't been applied yet — the verify script still has the same max_new_tokens and regex issues that fail on truncated think blocks. There are also three stale probe processes still running on deleted data. Before I can re-collect activations, I need to check what actually changed in the verify script at 01:28:22.
```

Rowling\\\" vs \\\"Rowling\\\" → last-name fallback\\n    - \\\"1,000\\\" vs \\\"1000\\\" → number normalization\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    cand = normalize_answer(candidate)\\n    ref = normalize_answer(reference)\\n\\n    if not cand or not ref:\\n        return False\\n\\n    if cand == ref:\\n        return True\\n\\n    #     "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

_source line 1281 · 2026-05-21T17:35:22.391Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The verify script WAS fixed:

- Line 156: `enable_thinking=False` added to `apply_chat_template`
- `max_new_tokens=128` kept (which is fine since thinking is disabled — 128 tokens is enough for short factual answers without think blocks)

So BOTH bugs were fixed:
1. ✅ `collect_activations.py` — left-padding extraction fixed
2. ✅ `verify_parametric_knowledge.py` — `enable_thinking=False` added

But the verify data hasn't been RE-RUN yet. 

The real problem is that the existing verified labels are contaminated from the buggy `strip_think_tags` function, and even though the activation collection now extracts from the correct position, it's still using those same noisy labels. This means the Qwen3 probes will continue to be trained on mislabeled data regardless of the extraction fix.

For Llama, the situation is cleaner since verification never involved thinking mode, so the labels are reliable and the new activations with correct extraction should produce trustworthy probe results.

The key blockers are: the Qwen3 verification needs to be re-run with the fixed code, there are three stale probe processes still consuming GPU resources that should be killed, and I should verify the GPU boundary compliance for the newly collected activations before proceeding further.
```

```text
**Think-tag contamination confirmation**: 680 out of 878 Qwen3 verified train entries contain the word "think" — 77.4% contaminated. This matches my earlier finding. 

The Qwen3 verified data hasn't been regenerated with the `enable_thinking=False` fix yet. The grep results showing 77.4% contamination could theoretically include natural uses of "think" in answers or questions, but that ratio matching my previous findings strongly suggests these are actual `&lt;think>` tags rather than coincidental word occurrences. Now I'm drafting the audit report for this batch.

The fixes were applied correctly across both scripts—left-padding in the activation collector and the thinking flag in the verification script. Both models had their English activations re-collected with the corrected code, and the old buggy files were properly cleaned up before rerunning. The main outstanding issue is that Qwen3's verified dataset still contains the contaminated entries and needs to be regenerated.

There are also three stale probe processes still consuming massive CPU resources on the overwritten data, and an old results file lingering in the Qwen3 directory that should be removed. Llama's English probes can proceed with the fresh activations, but Qwen3 remains blocked until the verification step is actually executed.
```

_source line 1323 · 2026-05-21T17:43:12.138Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings from the Qwen EN probe v2 results:

**probe_results.json** (new at 01:42):
- Best layer: 19, accuracy: **0.7017** (70.17%)
- p_binomial: 0.0 (highly significant)
- p_permutation: 0.0 (at layer 19 — permutation test was run on best layer)
- Pattern: gradual rise from layer 0 (~57%) to mid-layers (~70%), then gradual decline

**Old results (probe_qwen_en.log)**: layer 17, acc=0.6303 (63.03%)


**New results (probe_results.json)**: layer 19, acc=0.7017 (70.17%)

The left-padding bug fix yielded a 7.14 percentage point improvement. I need to verify the v2 log file to confirm these are the latest results, and the EN→EN performance of 70.17% puts us in the conditional pass range for the MVP gate.

The real insight here is that we're working with contaminated labels — 77.4% of the verified data still has thinking-tag contamination from Qwen3. This noise is actually masking the true signal strength. If I re-run verification with thinking disabled to clean up the labels, the accuracy could push toward 80% or higher. The layer-by-layer progression shows steady improvement from layer 0 (56.7%) through the mid-layers, peaking around layer 19, which suggests the model is learning the distinction progressively. The gap between my results and the literature benchmarks could stem from label contamination in the dataset or simply that ConflictQA presents a genuinely harder problem than the synthetic conflicts used in prior work. I should check the v2 logs and investigate what's happening with those stale processes.
```

</details>
